# Dental Bridges Reno

## Adjacent Teeth for a Complete Smile

Remember, tooth loss doesn’t just affect parts of your smile that are isolated from each other. The condition can easily cause missing adjacent teeth, giving your grin a wide gap. It’s for this reason that Smile Design Implant Centers offer dental bridges here in Reno. Through these special prosthetics, our team can get rid of your smile’s unwanted gaps and improve your oral health. Learn more about them by reading below, or [book a consultation](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/request-an-appointment.html) in the coming days!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Dental Bridges?

-   Implant Surgery Handled Entirely In-House
-   Digital and Mess-Free Dental Impressions
-   Quality Materials & Results

## What is a Dental Bridge?

![A dentist working on a dental bridge](assets/images/bridges-1.jpg)

Like a denture or a dental implant, a dental bridge is a device that replaces lost teeth. It typically consists of varied materials fused to either side of an artificial tooth (i.e., a “pontic”). This design lets it restore one to four missing neighboring teeth, depending on the patient’s needs.

The best candidates for dental bridges lack adjacent teeth. After all, such patients can get full smiles with just one treatment; they won’t require much follow-up care. Qualifying for a dental bridge is otherwise easy; you only need decent oral health.

## Types of Dental Bridges

![A dental bridge and implant parts set against a blue background](assets/images/bridges-2.jpg)

Before you receive a dental bridge, our dentists will perform an oral exam to assess your smile. They’ll then suggest one of several types of dental bridges to you based on their findings. Indeed, the kinds they could recommend include:

### Traditional Dental Bridge

![A 3D illustration of a dental bridge](assets/images/bridges-3.jpg)

Per its name, the traditional dental bridge is the standard sort. It relies on two (or more) dental crowns fused to either side of an artificial tooth. Given this makeup, the restoration relies on nearby natural teeth (i.e., “abutments”) to remain stable.

The traditional bridge is suited for patients with small budgets, as it normally costs little. In exchange, it permanently alters your existing teeth during the placement process.

### Implant Bridge

![A 3D illustration of an implant bridge](assets/images/bridges-4.jpg)

Compared to the traditional kind, an implant bridge is a more permanent prosthetic. It relies on dental implants – not dental crowns and abutments – to secure itself to your smile. To that extent, the restoration is highly secure and tends to last a few decades.

While an implant bridge doesn’t remove any enamel, it’s the costlier type. You should consider your budget before you fully commit to it.

## The Benefits of Getting a Dental Bridge

![A happy, elderly man sitting in a dental chair](assets/images/bridges-5.jpg)

Due to how it works, a well-made dental bridge offers benefits like:

-   **A Natural Appearance** – Dental bridges use prosthetic teeth made of porcelain, so they blend seamlessly with your smile.
-   **Better Oral Health** – Since it fills the gaps between teeth, a dental bridge reduces the spread of harmful mouth bacteria. The device also stops your remaining pearly whites from tilting.
-   **Easier Meals** – The replacement teeth in a dental bridge are durable, so much so that they help you chew many foods.
-   **Enduring Results** – An average dental bridge lasts roughly 5-15 years, whereas well-kept ones work for 20+ years.

# Teeth Grinding Treatment Reno

## Treating Teeth Grinding at Its Source

Chronic teeth grinding, also known as bruxism, is a condition in which the jaw is placed under stress. This stress can cause the jaw to clench and grind against the teeth. Bruxism causes wear on the teeth as well as pain in the jaw and facial muscles. Other symptoms include earaches, headaches, and sore and sensitive teeth.

Bruxism is commonly caused when the top and bottom rows of teeth are unable to meet properly, although it can also occur due to stress, jaw injuries, and other underlying issues. This could be due to problems with the alignment of the bite or the shape and structure of the jaw and skull. A misaligned bite can cause the teeth to grind against each other in an attempt to find the proper alignment. This constant motion can wear down and destroy tooth enamel. Over time, it can cause severe damage to both the structure of the teeth and the health of surrounding tissues, such as the gums.

In some cases, an orthodontist can be able to provide a simple treatment to help correct the alignment of the bite. For example, a retainer can be used to prevent the upper and lower jaws from shifting back into their proper positions during sleep. Orthodontists are also capable of conducting a thorough evaluation to determine if a more invasive surgical procedure is required to correct the distortion. Options for teeth grinding treatment in Reno may include adjusting the position of the maxillary bones by means of orthodontic treatment, oral surgery, or both.

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Teeth Grinding Treatment?

-   Pressure-Free Complimentary Consultation
-   In-Office 3D Printer Allows for Same-Day Solutions
-   Numerous Flexible Financing Options Available

![](assets/images/tgrinding-booklet.png)

## The Guide Will Show You

-   Teeth Grinding Solutions
-   Teeth Grinding Causes
-   How We Can Help!
-   And More!

## Who Is at Risk of Teeth Grinding?

![Woman cringing in bed before teeth grinding treatment in Reno](assets/images/teeth-grinding-treatment-1.webp)

People who grind their teeth are most commonly in their late teens and early 20s, but it affects people of all ages. Chronic teeth grinding is commonly associated with anxiety and stress, but it can also develop due to misaligned or improperly spaced teeth, bite problems, or crookedness. Those who suffer from bruxism tend to clench their jaws together throughout sleep, which can take a toll on the muscles and joints of the jaw over time, leading to wear and tear.

Bruxism can cause significant damage to the teeth and gums if left untreated, so it’s important to visit your dentist if you’re experiencing any symptoms related to this condition. Your dentist will be able to determine the cause of the problem and create a treatment plan that will alleviate the symptoms of bruxism and protect your smile from further damage.

## How Is Teeth Grinding Diagnosed?

![Dentist pointing to computer screen showing x ray of teeth](assets/images/teeth-grinding-treatment-2.webp)

Dentists will closely examine your teeth for signs of wear and tear, such as chips and cracks. If your jaw muscles are sore, the dentist may order X-rays to check for damage to the jawbone and temporomandibular joints (TMJ). If necessary, you may be referred to a specialist for a diagnosis and treatment plan.

TMJ disorders are often misdiagnosed as other conditions. For example, patients with unilateral facial pain may be incorrectly diagnosed as having trigeminal neuralgia or migraines. Without proper treatment, these headaches can worsen over time and cause permanent damage to the joint and surrounding facial muscles. During your appointment, ask your dentist if you’re at risk for TMD and if you should see a specialist.

## Treatment for Teeth Grinding

![Man placing a mouthguard over his teeth](assets/images/teeth-grinding-treatment-3.webp)

Teeth grinding has several potential causes, including sleep apnea, anxiety, or an abnormal bite. Certain medications and certain types of behavior may also cause it. In any case, the effects can be severe.

The constant pressure put on your teeth can result in fractures, loosening of fillings, and even tooth loss. Over time, bruxism can also lead to jaw pain, headaches, and other painful conditions. Fortunately, there are several ways to address this problem.

One of the most common treatment options is an oral appliance (also known as a nightguard). This custom-made device fits over your teeth and prevents them from contacting each other during sleep, reducing jaw pain, stopping rapid wear-and-tear of your molars, and preserving your oral health.

Another option is to restore damaged teeth with a dental crown. A crown completely covers a damaged tooth. It prevents the enamel from chipping away and protects the roots of the tooth as well. Crowns can be used to hold together cracked or damaged teeth and to restore the appearance of your smile.

# Gum Disease Treatment Reno

## Restoring the Health of Your Smile’s Foundation

Your teeth are certainly an integral part of your smile; however, did you know that your gums play an equal role in maintaining excellent oral health? Without these soft tissues to hold your teeth in place, they wouldn’t be able to function as intended and would also be vulnerable to all sorts of issues. This is why gum disease is such a menace; this condition is actually the leading cause of tooth loss worldwide, and without intervention, it can wreak havoc on one’s smile as well as the rest of the body. Luckily, it's also something that our team can help with! To learn more about our options for gum disease treatment in Reno or to schedule an appointment, please contact our dental office.

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Gum Disease Treatment?

-   Qualified Staff with Years of Experience
-   Advanced Dental Technology Ensures Optimal Outcomes
-   Numerous Flexible Financing Options Available

## Scaling & Root Planing

![Illustrated dental scaler clearing plaque from teeth during gum disease treatment](assets/images/gum-disease-1.webp)

Scaling and root planing treatment is one of the go-to methods for addressing advanced gum disease. This entails a thorough cleaning both above and below the gumline, ensuring that all of the problematic bacteria are cleared out. The other key part of the process involves smoothing the rough surfaces of your tooth roots, to prevent future reinfection and to help the gum tissue healthily reattach itself.

## Chao Pinhole Technique

![Illustrated mouth with receding gums](assets/images/gum-disease-2.webp)

When gum recession occurs, the root structures of the teeth become exposed, meaning that decay and other problems can affect the teeth underneath the gumline. The Chao Pinhole Technique is a breakthrough treatment for gum recession, and unlike traditional grafting techniques, this method is incision and suture-free, nor does it require donor tissue. This means less discomfort for patients after treatment, faster recovery times, and of course, optimal results!

# Restorative Dentistry Reno

## Repairing Dental Damage with Reliable Restorations

Did you know that if just one of your teeth is cracked, decayed, or otherwise damaged, your entire dental health is technically at risk? Not to mention, your oral functionality is compromised to some degree, also impacting your self-esteem. Thankfully, restoring and repairing teeth is a simple matter for our team here at Smile Design Implant Centers. We offer a wide range of restorative dentistry services capable of addressing all kinds of needs; just contact our dental office in Reno to learn more!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Restorative Dentistry?

-   Natural-Looking, Customized Restorations
-   Skilled Dental Team with Years of Experience
-   Numerous Flexible Financing Options

## Dental Crowns

![Tweezers holding a dental crown](assets/images/restorative-dentistry-1.webp)

Dental crowns are truly one of the most capable and versatile treatments in all of restorative dentistry; their uses are practically endless. Made from durable, natural-looking materials such as zirconia, they’re most often used to protect damaged or decayed teeth from future harm and are typically used following root canal treatment to preserve the affected tooth. They can even replace missing teeth entirely in certain circumstances, with the help of a dental implant. At our Reno dental office, we can provide personalized, realistic-looking crowns capable of lasting for several years.

## Tooth-Colored Fillings

![Illustrated tooth with a white filling](assets/images/restorative-dentistry-2.webp)

Tooth decay and cavities are usually resolved with fillings; however, the dental fillings of the past have often been made from silver and grey-colored metal alloys that don’t look natural whatsoever. We’re thrilled to step away from this method and use natural-looking, tooth-colored materials instead! These fillings are just as durable and just as effective, if not more so, at treating cavities and promoting future oral wellness.

# Cosmetic Dentist Reno

## Helping You to Realize Your Smile’s Dazzling Potential

Nobody should ever feel unhappy or unsatisfied with their smile’s appearance; after all, our smiles are important assets that help us make meaningful connections with others! That said, you also shouldn’t feel discouraged or ready to give up if your smile needs some work; cosmetic dentistry is certain to offer a life-changing solution. If you’re curious about receiving cosmetic dentistry in Reno to help you realize your smile’s true potential, just give our office a call; we can’t wait to work with you!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Cosmetic Dentistry?

-   Personalized Dental Care to Meet Unique Needs
-   Skilled Dental Team with Keen Eye for Detail
-   Multiple Flexible Financing Options Available

## Veneers

![Several veneers on table next to dental mirror](assets/images/cosmetic-dentistry-1.webp)

Veneers are small coverings made from porcelain that can be directly bonded to the surfaces of teeth with aesthetic flaws. They’re ideal for addressing chips or cracks in teeth, gaps between teeth, staining that is resistant to whitening, and other types of issues. Although the method for receiving veneers does require some slight alteration to the existing teeth (a small amount of enamel must be removed), this is not a concern at all and ultimately ensures the future success of the veneers.

[Learn More About Veneers](veneers.html)

## Metal-Free Dental Restorations

![Illustrated row of teeth with white filling material](assets/images/cosmetic-dentistry-2.webp)

In the past, dental restorations were often made using a silver-colored material called metal amalgam, which was actually a mixture of various types of metal alloys. While effective and durable, these kinds of restorations are far from natural-looking and also tend to be reactive to temperatures, among other small flaws. Here at Smile Design Implant Centers, we’re pleased to offer metal-free restorations made from beautiful, biocompatible materials like zirconia. With the right care, these can last for years, helping you to achieve optimal oral health and beauty for the foreseeable future.

## Cosmetic Dental Bonding

![Illustration of dental bonding material being applied to upper front tooth](assets/images/cosmetic-dentistry-3.webp)

Cosmetic bonding, sometimes referred to as dental bonding, is one of the most versatile cosmetic dentistry treatment options that we offer here in our Reno dental office. It’s capable of tackling all kinds of aesthetic issues, including chipped or cracked teeth, misshapen teeth, stubborn areas of staining, and more. It involves applying a small amount of composite resin to the tooth or teeth in question, and molding and shading it as needed to ensure a seamless, natural appearance. This resin is hardened with a special curing light and can last for several years before it needs to be touched up or replaced, which is just as easy of a process as the first time. 

## Teeth Whitening

![Dental patient having her teeth professionally whitened](assets/images/cosmetic-dentistry-4.webp)

Very few people have naturally white teeth; not to mention, some smiles have the tendency to look duller after years of wear and consuming certain foods and beverages. Luckily, teeth whitening treatment is often an ideal way to reverse this and ensure dazzling teeth for many years to come. Here at Smile Design Implant Centers, we proudly offer both in-office and take-home teeth whitening treatments. Each option is personalized for your specific needs and capable of helping you achieve dazzling results. That said, many of our patients tend to prefer our take-home kits, as the convenience of whitening on your own schedule and from the comfort of your own home simply can’t be beaten!

[Learn More About Teeth Whitening](teeth-whitening.html)

## Gum Recontouring/Crown Lengthening

![Illustrated dental laser treating an uneven gumline](assets/images/cosmetic-dentistry-5.webp)

Do your gums cover up more of your teeth than they’re supposed to? This issue is commonly referred to as a “gummy smile,” and though it might not seem like a huge deal, it can actually increase the risk of certain dental issues – not to mention, it can notably impact one’s self-esteem! That alone is reason enough to seek treatment like gum recontouring, also sometimes called crown lengthening. This process allows us to carefully reshape your gumline and remove excess gum tissue as needed, ensuring that your teeth are fully visible.

## Smile Makeovers

![Dental patient beaming at her new smile in mirror](assets/images/cosmetic-dentistry-6.webp)

Some patients have concerns that their smiles are in such abysmal states that cosmetic treatment simply isn’t worthwhile – but this is never the case! This is where smile makeovers truly shine; we can carefully consider your situation, evaluate your smile goals, and work with you to come up with a streamlined and effective treatment plan for getting your teeth back on track. We utilize virtual smile design as a part of this process, to ensure that you’re able to clearly see the exciting direction your smile is heading in!

[Learn More About Smile Makeovers](smile-makeover.html)

# Smile Makeover – Reno, NV

## Taking Flawed Smiles & Making Them Beautiful

Are you unhappy with your smile? Do you miss out on opportunities for professional improvement because you are self-conscious about your appearance? It’s time to change all of that with a [smile makeover](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/cosmetic-dentistry.html)! At Smile Design Implant Centers, our cosmetic dentists in Reno and specialists are here to make your pearly whites shine so that you can enjoy greater confidence and a showstopping smile. If you’re ready to change your appearance for good, [call](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/contact.html) to schedule a consultation today!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Smile Makeovers?

-   Dentists Provide Complimentary Consultations
-   Skilled Dental Specialists on Staff
-   Superior Dental Technology for Treatment Planning & Restoration

## How Does a Smile Makeover Work?

![Dentist discussing how a smile makeover works with patient ](assets/images/makeover-1.jpg)

Your smile makeover will differ from another patient’s because each smile is unique. A scheduled consultation with your cosmetic dentist is the first step, as it allows you to discuss your concerns and goals. If you have an idea of what you want your pearly whites to look like, feel free to bring in pictures to show our team.

Once we examine your mouth, face, skin tone, and overall oral health, we will build a treatment plan that aligns with your goals. This will determine which services we believe will deliver the results you desire – veneers, teeth whitening, gum recontouring, metal-free dental restorations, cosmetic dental bonding, and more.

## The Benefits of Getting a Smile Makeover

![Young man smiling happily after a smile makeover ](assets/images/makeover-2.jpg)

Agreeing to receive a smile makeover comes with numerous benefits, such as:

-   A seamless process that allows our team to combine multiple services into a single plan, as opposed to treating each problem area individually.
-   Enhanced confidence in the way you look and feel, which can have a positive impact on your physical, mental, and emotional health.
-   No longer seeing cosmetic flaws when you look in the mirror, but instead, seeing a younger-looking smile that gives you a reason to smile.
-   Improve oral health because specific treatments (i.e., metal-free dental restorations) allow you to eat more comfortably and use your teeth normally. They also help close gaps and create a healthier environment.

## Understanding the Cost of a Smile Makeover

![Woman pointing to her new smile ](assets/images/makeover-3.jpg)

The cost of a smile makeover will vary depending on the complexity of your flaws and which services you receive. During your consultation, we will discuss the details of your treatment plan, including the factors that contribute to the total cost. We’ll then go over the different ways you can pay for treatment since dental insurance is unlikely to cover the cost. This may include applying for flexible financing through CareCredit, Proceed Finance, or iCreditworks.

# Veneers – Reno, NV

## Address Aesthetic Imperfections and Boost Your Confidence

Do you have one or more dental flaws making you feel self-conscious about your appearance? If you’re worried about being judged for the state of your smile, you might develop anxiety or depression, or try to avoid spending time with others.

Our dedicated team in Reno can address aesthetic imperfections and boost your confidence with customized veneers. Continue reading to learn more about this popular [cosmetic](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/cosmetic-dentistry.html) treatment and who it can help, and please don’t hesitate to contact us for additional information or to schedule a consultation.

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Veneers?

-   Customizable, Tooth-Colored Materials
-   Advanced Technology to Enhance Patient Comfort
-   Active Members of the American Dental Association

## What Are Dental Veneers?

![Closeup of a smile with shade guide held close to teeth](assets/images/veneers-1.jpg)

Veneers are thin shells, typically made from resilient porcelain, that are bonded to the front surfaces of your teeth to cover imperfections. These restorations are also carefully customized to match your natural smile perfectly in terms of shade, size, and shape for beautiful results you can feel proud of.

This versatile treatment can be used to address a single issue or multiple concerns simultaneously, including:

-   Chipped or cracked enamel.
-   Stubborn stains that can’t be brushed away.
-   Mild gaps between teeth.
-   Slight misalignments.
-   Worn-down or stubby teeth.

## The Process of Getting Veneers

![Woman in dental chair smiling with shade guide held to her teeth](assets/images/veneers-2.jpg)

Although this procedure can potentially benefit anyone who is unhappy with the way their teeth look, that doesn’t mean it’s the best way to meet everyone’s unique needs. Before proceeding, you must consult one of our experts to ensure you’re a good candidate. If we find any signs of pre-existing issues, like gum disease or cavities, we must fix them before proceeding with cosmetics.

Then, we must prepare your teeth by removing a small amount of enamel so your veneers fit correctly. We will likely apply a local anesthetic to numb the area before this preparation to help you feel comfortable, and also offer multiple options for sedation if needed. Next, we’ll make impressions of your teeth and send them to a lab, which will use the information to create your customized shells.

Our team will likely place temporary veneers to safeguard your grin until the finished restorations are delivered. Once they arrive, we’ll schedule a final fitting to cement them in place and send you home with a beautifully restored smile!

## The Benefits of Veneers

![Woman holding coffee looking out café window smiling](assets/images/veneers-3.jpg)

There are several excellent reasons to get veneers in Reno, including:

-   **Versatile solution.** Our experts can position your restorations to mask one or several issues with a single treatment.
-   **Protect your teeth.** The shells are strong enough to help shield the underlying teeth from additional cracks and breaks, and is coated with a glaze so durable that it resists damage and discoloration.
-   **Enhanced appearance.** You’ll look and feel like the most attractive, confident version of yourself.
-   **Long-lasting results.** These covers have been known to last 20+ years if cared for correctly.
-   **Easy maintenance.** All that’s required to keep them in good shape is a twice-daily at-home dental hygiene routine and scheduling routine checkups every six months.

## Understanding the Cost of Veneers

![Pink piggy bank on top of large model tooth with orange background](assets/images/veneers-4.jpg)

Most dental insurance policies don’t cover cosmetic procedures that are intended to improve your appearance more than your oral health, like veneers. As a result, many patients worry they can’t afford them.

If you’re concerned that your desired service is outside of your budget, we’ve got your back! We proudly offer flexible financial payment plans through various third-party financers, such as CareCredit, Proceed Finance, and iCreditworks. If your application is approved, these options allow you to divide your total invoice into more manageable installments over a designated period that won’t break the bank.

# Teeth Whitening – Reno, NV

## Brighten Your Smile and Boost Your Confidence

If you’re feeling self-conscious about stained or blemished teeth, you might feel tempted to try over-the-counter whitening products to reduce discoloration. However, these options may produce inconsistent results or cause sensitivity.

Meanwhile, our experts in Reno can provide professional whitening services to brighten your smile and boost your confidence. Continue to read more about this popular [cosmetic](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/cosmetic-dentistry.html) treatment to help decide if it’s right for you!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Teeth Whitening?

-   Team of Board-Certified Experts
-   Member of the American Academy of Cosmetic Dentistry
-   Flexible Financing Available

## Who is a Good Candidate for Teeth Whitening?

![Nose-to-shoulder view of man in white shirt smiling](assets/images/whitening-1.jpg)

Anyone unhappy with the tone of their teeth can benefit from professional whitening treatments. However, that doesn’t mean that everyone is automatically a good candidate. You must consult one of our providers before proceeding to ensure that no pre-existing problems, like cavities or gum disease, interfere with the results.

Our team will examine your condition to determine the types of stains present and the severity of your condition before devising a customized treatment plan to meet your unique needs. For example, stains caused by consuming too many darkly pigmented foods often respond more quickly to topical bleaching than intrinsic discoloration in the lower layer of dentin, which may require repeated treatments or another procedure altogether.

## How Does Teeth Whitening Work?

![Closeup of a smile that’s half whitened to show results](assets/images/whitening-2.jpg)

Our team offers two different options to brighten your smile, and can recommend one of the following solutions to meet your needs after your initial consultation:

### Take-Home Teeth Whitening

![Woman smiling as she undergoes in-office teeth whitening](assets/images/whitening-3.jpg)

We understand that some patients prefer to lighten their teeth in the comfort of their own home, and are happy to provide all the supplies needed to obtain professional whitening results. For example, our take-home kits include ultra-concentrated gels designed to mitigate potential sensitivity as well as custom-fitted applicator trays for more comfortable and accurate results. Not only that, but many patients start to see results in as few as two weeks.

### In-Office Teeth Whitening

![Woman applying whitening gel to customized applicator tray](assets/images/whitening-4.jpg)

Our team in Reno also offers in-office whitening procedures for more immediate results. For this option, we apply the product to your teeth, then speed up the whitening process by exposing them to ultraviolet light. This is a popular solution for those who have an upcoming occasion, such as a wedding, reunion, or graduation, and want to look their best.

## Tips for Maintaining Your Brighter Smile

![Closeup of UV light being applied to someone’s teeth whitening](assets/images/whitening-5.jpg)

Professional teeth whitening can enhance your appearance and self-esteem, and it’s only natural to want to do everything you can to maintain the results. You can help preserve your brighter smile by:

-   **Implement a twice-daily dental hygiene routine.** Brushing and flossing morning and night removes plaque that gives darkly toned particles something to cling to.
-   **Repeat treatments as needed.** You might require periodic touch-ups to maintain the perfect shade.
-   **Choose foods carefully.** Avoiding common culprits known to stain, such as coffee, tea, berries, and wine, can help avoid future discoloration.

# SureSmile Clear Aligners Reno

## Unlock the Benefits of a Straighter, Healthier Smile

If you’re tired of living with misaligned or crooked teeth, or you suffer from malocclusion (bite problems), you might be wondering if SureSmile clear aligners can help – and you’ll be happy to hear that they can! This exciting, modern orthodontic treatment can surprisingly assist with a wide range of issues while offering increased comfort and unmatched discretion compared to traditional braces. To learn more about SureSmile clear aligners in Reno or to schedule a consultation, contact our dental office today!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for SureSmile Clear Aligners?

-   Highly-Customizable Treatment Plans
-   Several Flexible Financing Options Available
-   Faster Treatment Timeline Compared to Braces

## Clear Aligners vs. Traditional Braces

![Smiling young woman holding a Sure Smile clear aligner in Reno](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-1.webp)

When considering orthodontic treatment options, individuals often weigh the differences between clear aligners and traditional braces. Traditional braces consist of metal brackets affixed to the teeth, connected by wires, while SureSmile utilizes clear, removable aligners.

One primary distinction lies in aesthetics; SureSmile’s clear aligners offer a discreet option, ideal for individuals seeking a less noticeable treatment. Conversely, though more visible, traditional braces are effective for complex cases and may be preferable for those requiring extensive orthodontic correction.

SureSmile aligners are also removable, facilitating easier oral hygiene and dietary flexibility, while braces are fixed throughout the treatment duration. Despite these differences, both options can achieve remarkable results in correcting misalignments and bite issues, with the choice often depending on individual preferences, treatment goals, and the recommendation of an orthodontic professional.

## How Does SureSmile Work?

![Dental patient having digital impressions taken of their teeth](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-2.webp)

SureSmile works through a series of clear, custom-made aligners that gradually shift your teeth into the desired position. The process typically starts with a consultation with an orthodontist at Smile Design Implant Centers. During this visit, they will assess your teeth and bite to determine if SureSmile is the right option for you.

Once you proceed with SureSmile, your provider will take a digital impression of your mouth. These images are used to create a 3D model of your mouth and design a personalized treatment plan. This plan maps out the gradual movement of your teeth throughout your journey to help track your progress.

Based on the treatment plan, a series of clear aligners are custom-made for you. Each aligner is slightly different from the last, applying gentle pressure to specific teeth to gradually move them into their desired positions. You'll typically wear each set of aligners for two weeks before switching to the next set in the series.

You'll visit your provider periodically throughout the treatment to ensure your progress is on track. They may make adjustments to your treatment plan as needed. Most SureSmile treatments last anywhere from six months to two years, depending on the case's complexity.

SureSmile offers a discreet and convenient alternative to traditional braces. It allows you to straighten your teeth without the noticeable appearance of metal brackets and wires. Additionally, because the aligners are removable, you can still enjoy your favorite foods and easily maintain your regular oral hygiene routine.

## The Benefits of SureSmile

### Discreet Appearance

![Close up of person placing clear aligner over their teeth](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-3.webp)

SureSmile aligners in Reno are made from a clear, transparent plastic material, making them virtually invisible when worn. Unlike traditional braces with metal brackets and wires, clear trays offer a more discreet orthodontic method, allowing users to feel more confident about their smile during treatment.

### Comfortable Experience

![Woman placing a clear aligner over her teeth](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-4.webp)

SureSmile aligners are custom-made to fit snugly over the teeth, with smooth edges that minimize irritation to the gums and cheeks. Unlike metal braces, which may cause discomfort or soreness, particularly after adjustments, SureSmile trays provide users with a more comfortable orthodontic experience.

### Removability

![Young woman smiling while brushing her teeth](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-5.webp)

One significant advantage of SureSmile is that the aligners are removable. This feature allows users to easily remove the aligners for eating, drinking, brushing, and flossing. As a result, there are no dietary restrictions associated with SureSmile, and maintaining good oral hygiene is simpler compared to traditional braces.

### Improved Oral Hygiene

![Brunette woman smiling and holding a clear aligner](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-6.webp)

Because SureSmile aligners are removable, users can maintain their regular oral hygiene routine without any added difficulty. Unlike traditional braces, which can make brushing and flossing more challenging due to brackets and wires, clear aligners allow for easier cleaning of the teeth and gums, potentially reducing the risk of cavities and gum disease.

## Tips for Maintaining Oral Health with SureSmile

![Woman holding a glass of water](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-7.webp)

-   Brush your teeth after every meal and snack before reinserting your aligners.
-   Use a soft-bristled toothbrush and fluoride toothpaste to gently clean your teeth, including all surfaces and along the gumline.
-   Floss daily to remove plaque and food particles from between your teeth.
-   Rinse your aligners with water when you remove them from your mouth.
-   Soak your aligners periodically in a specialized cleaning solution to remove bacteria and prevent odors.
-   To prevent the discoloration of your teeth and aligners, minimize your consumption of staining foods and beverages such as coffee, tea, red wine, and tomato sauce.
-   Stay hydrated by drinking plenty of water throughout the day to help flush away bacteria and food particles.
-   Schedule regular dental checkups and cleanings with your dentist throughout your treatment.
-   Store your aligners in their case when not in use to protect them from damage and contamination.
-   To ensure the success of your treatment, follow your dentist’s instructions for wearing and caring for your aligners.

## SureSmile vs. Invisalign: How Are They Different?

![Man smiling at his dentist who is holding a clear aligner](assets/images/suresmile-clear-aligners-8.webp)

Most people immediately recognize Invisalign, as they’re the brand that first launched clear orthodontic aligners in 1997. However, many different brands have since become available, using more durable, patented materials to try to improve the clear aligner experience for patients, like SureSmile. Although SureSmile didn’t start making their trays until 2007, they’ve quickly risen in the ranks in clear aligners treatments and are one of the most popular Invisalign alternatives on the market.

SureSmile treatment utilizes virtually all of the same technology and methods of straightening teeth as Invisalign, but one great perk of SureSmile is that the treatment is typically less costly. SureSmile trays are also crafted from a specially manufactured plastic that’s often regarded as being more comfortable, clear, and effective.

Whether you're an adult striving for a confident smile, a teen navigating adolescence with grace, or a professional seeking a polished appearance, Invisalign can empower you to unlock the smile of your dreams. Call (775) 924-1871 to schedule a consultation today and take the first step towards achieving the confident, beautiful smile you deserve!

# Sedation Dentist Reno

## Making Dentistry Comfortable & Enjoyable

These days, it’s pretty common to feel reluctant, or even frightened, about visiting the dentist; it’s estimated that 15% of people worldwide suffer from this “dentophobia,” or debilitating fear of dental treatment. But when this condition causes you to neglect your smile or skip dental visits, it becomes a big problem! That’s why our team is excited to offer sedation dentistry; it’s a surefire way to promote a positive and comfortable experience in the dental chair. To learn more about how we can utilize sedation dentistry in Reno to make your next visit the smoothest appointment of your life, just give our office a call today!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Sedation Dentistry?

-   Comfortable Office with Patient-Friendly Amenities
-   Accommodating and Qualified Dental Staff
-   Numerous Sedation Options Available

![](assets/images/sedation-booklet.png)

## The Guide Will Show You

-   Sedation Options
-   Eligbility Factors
-   Sedation FAQ's
-   And More!

## IV Dental Sedation

![Sedation dentist in Reno adjusting an I V bag](assets/images/sedation-dentistry-1.webp)

Does the thought of sitting in a dental chair cause you overwhelming anxiety? If you’ve delayed important care due to fear or discomfort, you’re not alone – and there’s a solution. We’re proud to offer IV sedation, a safe and highly effective way to help you relax and feel at ease during your appointment. Whether you struggle with dental phobia or are preparing for an extensive procedure, this option can make your visit more comfortable from start to finish.

Read More

### What Is IV Dental Sedation?

![](assets/images/iv-sedation-1.jpg)

IV sedation is the deepest form of conscious sedation available in a dental setting. The sedative is administered directly into your bloodstream through an IV line, allowing it to take effect almost immediately. Although you’ll remain conscious and able to respond to verbal cues, most patients feel so deeply relaxed that they remember very little (if anything) about the procedure afterward.

During treatment, your heart rate, blood pressure, and oxygen levels will be continuously monitored to ensure your safety. The dosage can also be adjusted in real time, offering a customized level of sedation based on your needs and comfort level. This makes IV sedation especially valuable for patients undergoing long or complex dental procedures, such as wisdom tooth extractions or dental implants.

### Who Is a Good Candidate for IV Dental Sedation?

![](assets/images/iv-sedation-2.jpg)

IV dental sedation may be recommended for patients who:

-   Suffer from severe dental anxiety or fear
-   Are undergoing lengthy or surgical dental procedures
-   Have a strong gag reflex that interferes with treatment
-   Struggle to become fully numb with local anesthesia

Before administering IV sedation, our team will conduct a thorough medical review to ensure you’re a good candidate. This includes checking for any allergies and reviewing your overall health to make sure IV sedation is a safe and appropriate option.

### The Benefits of IV Dental Sedation

![](assets/images/iv-sedation-3.jpg)

If you’re unsure whether IV sedation is right for you, consider these important benefits:

-   It promotes a deep sense of calm and relaxation during your visit
-   Most patients have little to no memory of their treatment
-   Sedation begins quickly and can be adjusted at any time during the procedure
-   It allows for multiple treatments in one visit, reducing the need for future appointments
-   It can help you finally receive the care you’ve been avoiding, improving your overall oral health
-   When monitored by trained professionals, IV sedation is extremely safe and effective

At the end of the day, your comfort and safety are our top priorities. If dental fear has held you back from achieving a healthy smile, IV sedation could be the key to a more positive and stress-free dental experience!

## Oral Conscious Sedation

![Person holding a glass of water in one hand and a few pills in the other](assets/images/sedation-dentistry-2.webp)

Oral conscious sedation is normally recommended for patients who are unable to benefit from nitrous oxide, those with moderate dental-related anxiety, and those who are undergoing more complex procedures. It involves taking prescribed medicine before your appointment so that by the time you’re seated for treatment, you’ve started feeling its effects. You’ll still be conscious and responsive, but you’ll feel totally relaxed and at ease, albeit also a little drowsy.

## Nitrous Oxide Sedation

![Dental patient wearing a nitrous oxide mask over their nose](assets/images/sedation-dentistry-3.webp)

Often referred to as “laughing gas,” nitrous oxide has seen use in modern medicine for quite some time now, offering patients a way to comfortably endure virtually all types of dental treatments. Despite its name, it doesn’t cause patients to laugh; rather, it causes them to enter a euphoric and calm state that makes dental care easier for all parties involved. Patients are still conscious and responsive to verbal and physical stimuli. It’s among the best ways of addressing dentophobia and is generally quite safe and effective for all patients.

## Benefits of Sedation Dentistry

![Woman relaxing and leaning back in dental chair](assets/images/sedation-dentistry-4.webp)

Some patients can benefit from sedation during dental procedures. Here are some common benefits:

-   It allows them to relax so they can receive treatment without experiencing any stress or anxiety.
-   It helps them remain still and comfortable during treatment.
-   During the procedure, the patient remains awake but feels less pain. This makes it possible for the dentist to complete treatments that otherwise would not be possible due to a patient’s physical discomfort and pain.
-   The patient is still able to breathe independently, which means that they have no risk of asphyxiation.
-   Certain forms of sedation are safe to use in combination with local anesthesia, allowing the dentist to provide even more effective pain management for patients. For example, nitrous oxide can be combined with local anesthetics to reduce the pain associated with an injection.
-   Sedation promotes faster healing after a dental procedure. That’s especially important for patients who need to undergo a complex treatment plan that involves several appointments. With sedation, they can undergo more of the procedures they need in a single visit without requiring additional appointments in the future.
-   There is very little to no downtime after using sedation during a dental appointment. Patients can return to their normal activities immediately after the procedure is completed.
-   Patients who have certain medical conditions require the use of sedation because the treatment is invasive. They should not put off dental treatment because they fear they will not be able to cope with the pain or anxiety of the procedure.
-   Sedation allows dentists to treat patients who are very young or very old. Children who haven’t visited a dentist before may be fearful and require the help of sedatives. Older adults may have difficulty staying still for long periods of time. Dentists may use sedation to help them perform necessary procedures without causing unnecessary pain and trauma to the patient.

## Sedation Dentistry FAQs

### How long does dental sedation last? 

How long the effects linger depends largely on the type of dental sedation you choose. For example, the effects of oral conscious sedation often last for the rest of the day, which is why patients are instructed to arrange for a trusted adult to drive them home, to avoid operating heavy machinery, and to focus on resting. In contrast, the effects of nitrous oxide dissipate within a matter of minutes. So, you don’t have to make any special arrangements – you can resume your usual routine right after your appointment!

### Will I remember anything with dental sedation? 

Even with more mild forms of sedation dentistry, like nitrous oxide, there’s a good chance that you won’t remember much about your time in the treatment chair. This memory loss effect has a positive, unexpected outcome for many patients: it helps decrease their dental-related anxiety moving forward. So, if you struggle with stress, worry, or fear surrounding dental care, don’t hesitate to schedule a consultation with us to find out if you’re a candidate for dental sedation. There’s a good chance that it’s the solution you’ve been looking for!

### Is sedation dentistry covered by insurance? 

There are some cases where sedation dentistry is covered, like if you have a disability that makes it impossible to get the preventive or restorative care you need without it. That said, it is considered a luxury in most cases and, therefore, is an expense paid for out-of-pocket. If you have any questions pertaining to your coverage, don’t hesitate to reach out to your provider directly or read through the fine print on your plan. You can also give us a call. Once we have your membership information, we can review your benefits and provide you with an estimate of your out-of-pocket expenses.

### Is sedation dentistry worth it? 

If you have been putting off getting the care you need – whether it’s your six-month checkup or a root canal – sedation dentistry is worth the investment. Not only will it ensure that you don’t feel any anxiety in the treatment chair, but it will also create a more positive association with dental care moving forward. So, even if the anxiety, stress, or worry you feel seems minor, don’t hesitate to talk to us about the calming solutions we offer. We’d be happy to answer any questions you have and help you determine which one is ideal for you.

### Can dental sedation make you sick? 

There is a higher risk of nausea with stronger forms of sedation, like oral conscious sedation. For that reason, patients are typically asked not to eat anything prior to their appointment. With nitrous oxide, that’s typically not a concern. That said, if you struggle with feeling nauseous, don’t hesitate to let us know so we can work with you to find a solution.

### Does dental sedation make you tell secrets? 

No, dental sedation won’t make you reveal any deep, dark secrets. In fact, it oftentimes has such a calming effect that patients doze off!

# Emergency Dentist Reno

## Ready To Help You Deal with the Unexpected

Life is full of unexpected, unpredictable events – some of which come in the form of a dental emergency! These situations can be quite alarming, especially when they involve your or a loved one’s smile. However, the very last thing you should do is panic; help is readily available here at Smile Design Implant Centers! Our emergency dentists in Reno proudly offer same-day treatment for injured smiles, so if you or someone close to you has suffered a dental disaster, contact our office at once!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Emergency Dentistry?

-   Same-Day Emergency Appointments Available
-   Friendly and Accommodating Dental Team
-   Numerous Sedation Options for Anxious Patients

## How We Treat Dental Emergencies

![Man looking concerned while talking on phone with emergency dentist in Reno](assets/images/emergency-dentistry-1.webp)

Dental emergencies do not get better on their own – in fact, the opposite often happens, and things progressively worsen without treatment! With that said, if you or someone you know suffers a dental emergency in Reno, here are three easy steps you can follow to get the help you need:

-   **Call Our Office Promptly –** Once you’ve realized you’re suffering a dental emergency, call our office and describe your symptoms. We’ll let you know if you need to come on that day, and also provide you with first-aid tips over the phone.
-   **Attend Your Appointment –** Once you’ve arrived at our dental office, we can quickly take you back and perform an emergency examination to see the extent of the damage and take X-rays if necessary. Rest assured, we’ll relieve any pain you might be feeling, too.
-   **Receive the Care You Need –** After we’ve taken a thorough look, we can begin planning your treatment. This might involve one or multiple different treatments, depending on your needs. However, we’re committed to ensuring you leave with a bright and pain-free smile on your face!

## The Most Common Dental Emergencies

Not every dental emergency causes outright pain; however, that doesn’t mean things aren’t serious! In fact, a lack of pain might be a sign of something more serious and sinister going on. You shouldn’t hesitate to call our Reno dental office and explain what happened; we’ll be able to provide you with some steps to take until you come in to see us. In the meantime, here’s a brief list of a few common dental emergencies that we can usually treat with ease:

[

### Toothaches

](#toothache)[

### Chipped Tooth

](#chipped)[

### Cracked Tooth

](#cracked)[

### Sensitivity to Hot & Cold

](#sensitive)[

### Knocked-Out Tooth

](#knocked)[

### Lost Filling or Crown

](#lost)[

### Broken Denture

](#broken)[

### Loose Tooth

](#loose)[

### Gums, Lips or Tongue Injury

](#injury)[

### Jaw Pain or Clenching

](#pain)[

### Something Stuck Between Teeth

](#stuck)

More to Explore

[Prosthodontics](prosthodontics.html) [Dental Implants](dental-implants.html) [Missing Teeth Replacement](missing-teeth.html) [Teeth Grinding Treatment](teeth-grinding-treatment.html) [Gum Disease Treatment](gum-disease-treatment.html) [Restorative Dentistry](restorative-dentistry.html) [Cosmetic Dentistry](cosmetic-dentistry.html) [SureSmile Clear Aligners](suresmile-clear-aligners.html) [Sedation Dentistry](sedation-dentistry.html) [View Our Services](our-services.html)

### Toothaches  

**Is My Toothache a Dental Emergency?** Toothaches shouldn’t be ignored. That said, they often don’t require same-day care either. Ultimately, the urgency of the situation depends largely on the symptoms you’re experiencing and how severe they are.

**How You Should Handle a Toothache** There are a few ways you can alleviate discomfort and protect your tooth from further harm until your appointment with us, including using warm saltwater to rinse periodically, placing a cold compress against the outside of your cheek, and not eating anything crunchy.

**How We Treat Toothaches** Ultimately, how we treat your toothache will depend on what’s causing it. If it’s a small cavity, then a tooth-colored filling may be the answer. If it’s a considerable crack, then a dental crown may be needed instead.

### Chipped Teeth  

**Is a Chipped Tooth a Dental Emergency?** Sometimes, same-day care isn’t needed. The best way to tell if you need to schedule an emergency appointment is by assessing the damage and your symptoms. If the chip is significant and you’re in a lot of pain, then don’t hesitate to call us.

**How You Should Handle a Chipped Tooth** Rinsing your mouth with clean, room-temperature water will help remove any lingering particles and debris. When you’re done, place some sugar-free gum or dental wax over your tooth to protect the soft tissue in your mouth, your lips, and your tongue from getting cut as well.

**How We Treat Chipped Teeth** Fortunately, restoring the missing structure is pretty easy with cosmetic dental bonding or a veneer. The one that’s best for you will depend on a few factors, including the severity of the damage, which is why we can’t make a recommendation until we take a look at your tooth.

### Cracked Tooth  

**Is a Cracked Tooth a Dental Emergency?** Yes, especially if you can see the damage. The sooner you get in touch with our Reno dental team, the sooner we can restore your healthy, pain-free smile.

**How You Should Handle a Cracked Tooth** There are a few steps we recommend taking, starting with not chewing on that side of your mouth. Taking OTC pain medication, not drinking hot coffee, and avoiding sugary foods will also go a long way in alleviating any unpleasant symptoms you’re experiencing.

**How We Treat Cracked Teeth** Once you arrive at our office, our emergency dentist in Reno will alleviate any pain you’re experiencing, complete a comprehensive oral exam, and determine if your tooth can be saved. If it can’t, then they will carefully extract the tooth and replace it with a custom dental implant.

[LEARN MORE ABOUT TOOTH EXTRACTIONS](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/tooth-extractions.html)

[LEARN MORE ABOUT DENTAL IMPLANTS](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/dental-implants.html)

### Very Sensitive Teeth

**Are Very Sensitive Teeth a Dental Emergency?** Dental sensitivity is more common than you may think. It can be the side-effect of enamel erosion or the cause of something more serious, like dental damage, which is why it’s important that you visit an emergency dentist for an examination at your earliest convenience.

**How You Should Handle Very Sensitive Teeth:** If you experience chronic dental sensitivity caused by enamel erosion, using a special toothpaste could help ease your discomfort. However, if a more serious concern is causing your dental sensitivity in Reno, you may need more extensive intervention, like a tooth-colored filling, dental crown, or root canal.

**How We Treat Very Sensitive Teeth:** If your dental sensitivity is the result of an infection or dental damage, our team will likely recommend restoring your tooth. If you suffer from enamel erosion, we can recommend a fluoride treatment at your next cleaning to help fortify your tooth enamel as well as suggest some products to use at home.

[Learn More About Restorative Dentistry](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/restorative-dentistry.html)

### Knocked-Out Tooth

**Is My Knocked-Out Tooth a Dental Emergency?** If you suffer from a knocked-out tooth in Reno, time is of the essence, making urgent care an immediate need. Call our office in Reno to schedule a same-day appointment.

**How You Should Handle a Knocked-Out Tooth:** If you’re able to locate your knocked-out tooth, pick it up (being sure that you only handle it by the crown, and avoid touching the roots), and gently rinse it with cool water. To keep the tooth viable, store it in a sealable container of milk or saltwater, or you can try to place it back in its socket. If your mouth is bleeding, use a clean washcloth or piece of sterile gauze to gently apply pressure to get it to stop.

**How We Treat Knocked-Out Teeth:** Our team will always try to preserve your natural teeth whenever possible. However, that can be tricky if a tooth has become completely dislodged and knocked-out. We will try to seat it back in its socket and see if it reintegrates with the jawbone. If it doesn’t, we may recommend replacing the tooth with a dental implant.

[Learn More About Dental Implants](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/dental-implants.html)

[Learn More About Other Tooth Replacement Options](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/missing-teeth.html)

### Lost Filling or Crown

**Is a Lost Filling or Crown a Dental Emergency?** A lost filling or dental crown in Reno requires urgent attention to prevent consequential oral health concerns from developing. Because of this, our team considers this issue a dental emergency. We advise you to call to schedule an appointment as soon as you’re able to.

**How You Should Handle a Lost Filling or Crown:** If you’re able to locate your lost filling or dental crown, pick it up, rinse it off, and bring it to your appointment with you. Avoid eating on the side of your mouth with your lost restoration, and rinse your mouth with salt water to keep the area clean.

**How We Treat Lost Fillings or Crowns:** It’s likely that we’ll need to replace the restoration. If it’s a dental filling, we can do that in-office the same day you see us. For a dental crown, we can place a temporary on your affected tooth until your permanent one arrives from our partnered dental laboratory. Then, as soon as we receive it, we will permanently cement your new restoration atop your tooth.

[Learn More About Restorative Dentistry](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/restorative-dentistry.html)

### Broken Denture

-   **Is My Broken Denture a Dental Emergency?** A damaged denture isn’t always an emergency, but you could hurt your gum tissues, bite alignment, or any remaining teeth if you wear them in their current condition. It’s best to contact our team in Reno for an appointment as soon as possible.
-   **How You Should Handle a Broken Denture:** Please don’t attempt to repair your restoration with super glue or other household supplies, which aren’t intended for oral use and can be toxic if ingested. Instead, call us and we’ll schedule an appropriate time to see you.
-   **How We Treat Broken Dentures:** We may be able to repair chips and cracks with composite resin or reline your dentures to repair the foundation. However, if they are completely broken, we may need to provide a new set or consider implant dentures.

[Learn More About Dentures](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/missing-teeth.html#denture)

[Learn More About Dental Implants](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/dental-implants.html)

### Loose Permanent Tooth

-   **Is a Loose Permanent Tooth a Dental Emergency?** A partially dislodged or extruded tooth always necessitates an emergency appointment because it’s at risk of falling out altogether. Contact our team, and we’ll likely schedule a same-day visit.
-   **How You Should Handle Loose Permanent Teeth:** Don’t poke or prod the loose tooth or eat anything too hard or sticky. Also, you can keep your mouth clean by rinsing with warm salt water, but you should avoid brushing and flossing until we can address it.
-   **How We Treat Loose Permanent Teeth:** Sometimes, a stint or other oral appliance can be placed to stabilize your tooth long enough to reintegrate with your jaw. Otherwise, we might need to consider extraction and replacement.

[Learn More About Tooth Extractions](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/tooth-extractions.html)

[Learn More About Replacing Missing Teeth](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/missing-teeth.html)

### Injury to the Gums, Lips, or Tongue

-   **Is an Injury to the Gums, Lips, or Tongue a Dental Emergency?** Cuts to the tender tissues inside your mouth tend to bleed, which can be alarming. Our team can address minor lacerations to restore your oral health.
-   **How You Should Handle an Injury to the Gums, Lips, or Tongue:** Rinse your mouth gently with water to clear away blood, dirt, and debris. Then, hold a clean washcloth or gauze to the wound firmly for several minutes to slow bleeding. You can also place an ice pack on that side of your face to reduce swelling.
-   **How We Treat Injuries to the Gums, Lips, or Tongue:** Our team can stitch the cut closed and provide antibiotics to prevent an infection. However, if the cut is severe or your bleeding doesn’t slow or stop after 10 minutes of applying pressure, please head to the nearest emergency room.

[Learn More About Sedation Dentistry](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/sedation-dentistry.html)

### Jaw Pain

-   **Is Jaw Pain a Dental Emergency?** There are several potential reasons for a sore or stiff jaw, and not all are urgent. If you experience persistent or severe pain, contact us so we can identify and treat the underlying issue.
-   **How You Should Handle Jaw Pain:** We might provide special care instructions over the phone for you to follow when you contact us. Then, you can usually safely address discomfort with over-the-counter pain medications or a cold compress.
-   **How We Treat Jaw Pain:** If you suspect your jaw is broken or dislocated, you should head to the closest emergency room immediately. Otherwise, our team can provide a nightguard or other oral appliance to alleviate your aches and protect your smile.

[Learn More About Teeth Grinding](https://www.smiledesignimplants.com/teeth-grinding-treatment.html)

### Something Stuck Between the Teeth

Having something lodged between your teeth, like a piece of food or other debris, isn’t always considered an emergency. However, you might accidentally injure your enamel or gum tissues if you try to pry it free with utensils, toothpicks, or other household items.

Contact our team and let us know what happened, and we’ll schedule an appointment to safely remove the item without harming your smile.

# Tooth Extractions Reno

## Removing Troubled Teeth to Safeguard Your Smile

It’s never easy to hear that you’re going to need to undergo tooth extraction; after all, the thought of parting ways with one of the pearly whites you’ve had your whole life isn’t a fun one to process. But you have nothing to fear or worry about; tooth extractions in Reno are only performed when there’s no other realistic way to save a tooth, or if it’s threatening your oral health. We can also ensure this process is as smooth and comfortable as possible for patients. Contact our office today to learn more about how we can remove troubled teeth to better safeguard your smile!

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Tooth Extractions?

-   Prosthodontist On-Staff
-   Accommodating, Patient-Friendly Team and Office
-   Numerous Tooth Replacement Options Available

![](assets/images/textractions-booklet.png)

## The Guide Will Show You

-   Step-By-Step Overview
-   Common Procedures
-   Common Questions
-   And More!

## When Is a Tooth Extraction Necessary?

![Illustration of impacted wisdom tooth pressing against adjacent tooth](assets/images/tooth-extractions-1.webp)

A tooth may need to be removed in the following scenarios:

-   Severe tooth decay or a cavity
-   Decay that has spread to the inner pulp of the tooth
-   Impacted wisdom teeth that can cause infection and pain
-   Fractured teeth that cannot be repaired with fillings or other restorations
-   Infection or gum disease that has caused severe damage to the soft tissue and bone surrounding the tooth
-   An abscess

## How Is a Tooth Extraction Performed?

![Red pills spilling out of a bottle](assets/images/tooth-extractions-2.webp)

To perform a tooth extraction, the dentist first numbs the area around the tooth with a local anesthetic. After the tooth is extracted, the dentist may put sutures to better assist in healing and prevent infection. Depending on how the extraction is done, you may need to have stitches removed after a few days or a few weeks.

Once the extraction is completed, you will be provided with instructions to follow for proper healing and steps to take to avoid complications like dry socket. In most cases, the recovery period lasts a few days to a week. The amount of time you need to wait before you can return to work or school will vary based on the type of work that you do.

For the first few days after the extraction, you should rest and avoid strenuous physical activity to reduce the risk of bleeding and possible injury. You should also avoid drinking from a straw for the first few days after the procedure to prevent dislodging the blood clot that has formed in the socket. Generally, over-the-counter painkillers are enough to manage any discomfort you may have following an extraction. However, if the pain persists, you should contact your dentist as soon as possible, as there may be more issues that need to be addressed.

## Types of Tooth Extraction

![Dental clasp holding an extracted tooth](assets/images/tooth-extractions-3.webp)

There are times when tooth removal may be your only viable option, especially when wisdom teeth need to be removed. When teeth cannot be saved and must be removed, your dentist may recommend one of the following procedures:

-   **Simple extraction** – The dentist will use a tool called an elevator to loosen your tooth gently from its socket without harming the surrounding bone or gums.
-   **Surgical extraction** – This procedure is recommended if the teeth are impacted or have broken off at the gum line. Your dentist will surgically remove all the damaged teeth and any surrounding tissue that may be infected.

# Wisdom Tooth Extractions Reno

## Preserving Your Oral Health by Removing Problem Teeth

Are you familiar with those pesky teeth that seem to appear out of nowhere during your late teens or early twenties? Yes, we're talking about wisdom teeth! These third molars can often cause more harm than good, leading to a common dental procedure known as wisdom tooth extractions. If you're wondering what this procedure entails, our team can help. Simply call our dental office to schedule a complimentary consultation for wisdom tooth extractions in Reno.

## Why Choose Smile Design Implant Centers for Wisdom Tooth Extractions?

-   Experienced Dentists On-Staff
-   Pressure-Free Complimentary Consultations
-   Numerous Financing Options Available

## What Are Wisdom Teeth?

![Illustrated impacted wisdom tooth pressing against adjacent molar](assets/images/wisdom-tooth-extraction-1.webp)

Wisdom teeth, also known as third molars, are the last set of teeth to emerge in your mouth. They typically make their appearance between the ages of 17 and 25, hence the name "wisdom" teeth. While some lucky individuals experience no issues with these extra teeth, for many people, wisdom teeth can be a source of trouble.

Unlike our ancestors who needed wisdom teeth to chew tough foods like roots and raw meat, modern humans have smaller jaws that may not have enough space to accommodate these additional molars. This lack of space often leads to impacted or partially erupted wisdom teeth.

Impacted wisdom teeth occur when they become trapped beneath the gum line or against neighboring teeth due to limited room for proper eruption. Partially erupted wisdom teeth can create pockets around them where bacteria can thrive, leading to infections and other dental problems.

These problematic situations caused by overcrowding or improper alignment are precisely why many dentists recommend having your wisdom teeth removed before complications arise. By proactively addressing potential issues early on through extraction, you can save yourself from future pain and discomfort!

## The Purpose of Wisdom Teeth Removal

![Woman wincing and holding her cheek in pain](assets/images/wisdom-tooth-extraction-2.webp)

Wisdom teeth, also known as third molars, are the last set of teeth to emerge in our mouths. However, more often than not, these late bloomers can cause a host of problems and discomfort. That's where wisdom teeth removal comes into play.

One of the main reasons for removing wisdom teeth is because they frequently become impacted. This means that there isn't enough space for them to properly grow and develop. As a result, they may grow at an angle or only partially emerge from the gum line. This can lead to pain, swelling, infection, and damage to surrounding teeth.

Another purpose of wisdom tooth removal in Reno is preventive care. Even if your wisdom teeth haven't caused any issues yet, removing them proactively can help prevent future complications down the road. By eliminating potential problems before they arise, you can save yourself from unnecessary pain and dental procedures later on.

Additionally, extracting problematic wisdom teeth can improve overall oral health and hygiene. When overcrowded or impacted wisdom teeth are removed in a timely manner by a skilled professional, it reduces the risk of developing cavities or gum disease around these hard-to-reach areas.

## When Is the Best Time to Remove Wisdom Teeth?

![Dentist showing a tablet screen to a patient](assets/images/wisdom-tooth-extraction-3.webp)

When it comes to wisdom teeth removal, timing is everything. But when exactly is the best time to have them extracted? The answer may vary depending on everyone’s unique circumstances.

In general, it is recommended to remove wisdom teeth during the late teenage years or early adulthood. This is because, at this age, the roots of the wisdom teeth are not fully developed yet, making extraction easier and less complicated.

However, every person's situation is different. Some individuals may experience no problems with their wisdom teeth and can go through life without needing them removed. Others may start experiencing pain or discomfort as their wisdom teeth begin to emerge.

If you're unsure whether your wisdom teeth need to be extracted, it's essential to consult with a dental professional who can evaluate your specific case. They will consider factors such as overcrowding, impaction, and potential damage to neighboring teeth before recommending extraction.

## How Are Wisdom Tooth Extractions Performed?

![Dentist and two assistants performing wisdom tooth extraction in Reno](assets/images/wisdom-tooth-extraction-4.webp)

Wisdom tooth removal in Reno is a common dental procedure to prevent potential problems caused by the eruption of these third molars. The process begins with a thorough examination and assessment by your dentist or oral surgeon.

To ensure your comfort during the procedure, local anesthesia or sedation may be administered. Once you're numb or relaxed, the dentist will carefully extract the impacted wisdom teeth. If necessary, an incision may be made in the gum tissue to access the tooth.

In some cases, wisdom teeth are deeply embedded in the jawbone and require extraction by sectioning them into smaller pieces for easier removal. This technique minimizes trauma to surrounding tissues.

After removing all affected teeth, any incisions are stitched closed using dissolvable sutures that eliminate the need for manual removal later on. Gauze pads are placed over extraction sites to control bleeding and promote blood clot formation.

The entire process of wisdom tooth removal in Reno usually takes about 45 minutes per tooth but can vary depending on complexity and individual factors. Your dentist will provide post-operative instructions regarding pain management, swelling reduction techniques, diet restrictions, and proper oral hygiene practices.

## Recovery After Wisdom Tooth Extractions

![Spoonful of salt on table next to glass of water](assets/images/wisdom-tooth-extraction-5.webp)

Once the procedure is complete, it's important to take proper care of yourself to ensure a smooth and speedy recovery.

Immediately following the surgery, you will experience some swelling and discomfort in the area. This is completely normal and can be managed with over-the-counter pain medication recommended by your dentist or oral surgeon. Applying an ice pack to your face for short intervals can also help reduce swelling.

During the first few days, it's essential to stick to a soft food diet that won't irritate the extraction site. This includes soups, yogurt, mashed potatoes, and smoothies. Avoid chewing on hard or crunchy foods as they may cause damage or dislodge any blood clots that have formed.

It's important to keep your mouth clean during this time, but be gentle when brushing near the extraction site. Your dentist may recommend using an antiseptic mouthwash or saltwater rinse to help prevent infection.

In terms of physical activity, it's best to avoid any strenuous exercise for at least 24-48 hours after surgery. Physical exertion can increase bleeding and slow down healing.

Every person's recovery process may vary slightly depending on their individual circumstances. It's essential to follow all post-operative instructions provided by your dental professional for optimal healing.

# Dental Insurance – Reno, NV

## How to Save When Visiting the Dentist

At Smile Design Implant Centers, we understand that many patients rely on their dental insurance coverage to help pay for necessary treatment. However, we believe it is important to treat individuals based on their oral healthcare needs. While the services we recommend may or may not be included as part of a plan’s covered benefits, we never want a patient to sacrifice high-quality treatment because of the stipulations set forth by dental insurance.

This is why we are not in-network with any insurance providers, but we will do our best to make sure each person receives the maximum reimbursement for administered treatment.

## How Dental Insurance Works

![Laptop and phone showing dental insurance information](assets/images/insurance-2.jpg)

Your decision to enroll in a private dental insurance plan means that you are entering into a contract with that company and agree to pay a monthly premium for access to your benefits. Unlike in-network dentists who engage in a similar agreement with various insurance companies – to lower fees for various services – our practice does not.

We are out-of-network, so patients like yourself must agree to pay the entire amount for treatment upfront. Although this might sound expensive, a key aspect to remember is that our team will take care of all the necessary paperwork that is required to file a claim. When we do, you can expect us to try and get you the highest reimbursement possible.

However, receiving any money back from your insurance company will only occur if you are undergoing preventive or restorative care, as cosmetic services are usually not covered by insurance.

### What is the Difference Between Dental and Medical Insurance?

![Middle-aged woman sitting on couch and smiling](assets/images/insurance-3.jpg)

To better understand the difference between dental and medical insurance, you can break them down into two categories: reactive and proactive. Medical insurance is more commonly used when a problem has already occurred. These companies offer greater coverage for invasive, complex, and extensive forms of treatment, not wellness exams.

Dental insurance, however, is proactive, offering you the opportunity to save when seeing your dentist for regular checkups and cleanings (about 100%). This lowers the risk of more serious dental problems (i.e., cavities, tooth decay, gum disease, etc.), which means keeping more money in your pocket while enjoying a healthier smile year-round.

### The Benefits of Choosing an Out-of-Network Dentist

![Dentist and male dental patient smiling](assets/images/insurance-4.jpg)

While there are advantages to seeing a dentist who is in-network with your dental insurance, there are also many great benefits to choosing an out-of-network dentist, such as:

-   **Unrestricted Care:** Instead of only administering treatment that is covered by your dental insurance plan, out-of-network dentists can provide a higher level of care by recommending services that your smile truly needs.
-   **Your Decision:** You no longer have to see a dentist who is in-network with your plan. Instead, you can choose someone who is out of network and provides the type of services you desire.
-   **Maintained Relationships:** It’s common for some dentists to end contractual agreements with certain insurance plans, so if your oral healthcare professional is no longer “in-network,” you can still choose to see them for exceptional care.
